<div class='question question'>

Simon and Fiona are sharing a cupboard. If Simon's things take up $\dfrac{2}{5}$ of the space and Fiona's things take up 
$\dfrac{2}{7}$ of the space, what fraction of the cupboard is empty? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Empty Space}      &= \text{Total space} - \text{Used space} \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {2} {5} + \dfrac {2} {7}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {2 \times 7} {5 \times 7} + \dfrac {2 \times 5} {7 \times 5}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {14} {35} + \dfrac {10} {35}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {14 + 10} {35}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {1} - \dfrac {24} {35} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1 \times 35} {1 \times 35} - \dfrac {24} {35} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {35 - 24} {35} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {11} {35}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {11} {35}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Empty Space}      &= \text{Total space} - \text{Used space} \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {2} {5} + \dfrac {2} {7}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {2 \times 7} {5 \times 7} + \dfrac {2 \times 5} {7 \times 5}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {14} {35} + \dfrac {10} {35}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= 1 - \bigg(\dfrac {14 + 10} {35}\bigg) \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {1} - \dfrac {24} {35} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1 \times 35} {1 \times 35} - \dfrac {24} {35} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {35 - 24} {35} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {11} {35}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {11} {35}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Tom uses $750 \ \text{g}$ of sugar from a $2.5 \ \text{kg}$ packet. How many kilograms of sugar are left? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sugar left}           &= \text{Total sugar} - \text{Sugar used by Tom} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 \ \text{kg} - 750 \ \text{g} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 \ \text{kg} - \dfrac {750} {1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 - \dfrac {750} {1000} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 - 0.75 \\\\
                            &= 1.75 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.75 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sugar left}           &= \text{Total sugar} - \text{Sugar used by Tom} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 \ \text{kg} - 750 \ \text{g} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 \ \text{kg} - \dfrac {750} {1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 - \dfrac {750} {1000} \\\\
                            &= 2.5 - 0.75 \\\\
                            &= 1.75 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.75 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Given that Tom eats $\dfrac{3}{5}$ of a cake and his friend, Jerry, eats $\dfrac{1}{3}$ of the cake, what fraction of the cake is left? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Cake left}   &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {3} {5} + \dfrac {1} {3} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {3 \times 3} {5 \times 3} + \dfrac {1 \times 5} {3 \times 5} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {9} {15} + \dfrac {5} {15} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {9 + 5} {15} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \dfrac {14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 15} {1 \times 15} - \dfrac {14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {15} {15} - \dfrac {14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {15 - 14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {15}
\end{aligned} 
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {15}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Cake left}   &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {3} {5} + \dfrac {1} {3} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {3 \times 3} {5 \times 3} + \dfrac {1 \times 5} {3 \times 5} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {9} {15} + \dfrac {5} {15} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \left( \dfrac {9 + 5} {15} \right) \\\\
                    &= 1 - \dfrac {14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 15} {1 \times 15} - \dfrac {14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {15} {15} - \dfrac {14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {15 - 14} {15} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {15}
\end{aligned} 
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {15}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Given that Tom's piece of cake weighed $150 \ \text{grams}$, what was the weight of the whole cake? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Weight of cake}      &= \dfrac {5} {3} \times 150 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 150} {3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times 50} {3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {3} \times 50} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                            &= 5 \times 50 \\\\
                            &= 250 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$250 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Weight of cake}      &= \dfrac {5} {3} \times 150 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 150} {3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times 50} {3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {3} \times 50} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                            &= 5 \times 50 \\\\
                            &= 250 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$250 \ \text {g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

The distance from Calais to Paris is $320 \ \text{km}$.

$5 \ \text{miles}$ is approximately $8 \ \text{kilometres}$.
 
Calculate the approximate distance in miles from Calais to Paris.  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance from Calais to Paris in miles}    &= 320 \ \text{km} \\\\
                                                 &= 8 \ \text{km} \times 40 \\\\
                                                 &= 5 \ \text{miles} \times 40 \\\\
                                                 &= 200 \ \text{miles}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$200 \ \text {miles}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance from Calais to Paris in miles}    &= 320 \ \text{km} \\\\
                                                 &= 8 \ \text{km} \times 40 \\\\
                                                 &= 5 \ \text{miles} \times 40 \\\\
                                                 &= 200 \ \text{miles}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

A book has $256$ pages with, on average, $33$ lines on each page and $9$ words on each line.

Find an estimate for the number of words in the book.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of words}          &= 256 \times 33 \times 9 \\\\
                                &= 8448 \times 9 \\\\
                                &= 76032
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$76032$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                                &= 8448 \times 9 \\\\
                                &= 76032
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

                    &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

</div>

<emptyline>
$

                    &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>23EFA4A7-D33C-4E18-906D-2E59D4849F16</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>eltham-college--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-2:1:11</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

It was reported recently that in an average lifetime of $70 \ \text{years}$, each
human is likely to swallow about $8$ spiders while sleeping. Supposing
that the population of UK is $60 \ \text{millions}$, what is the best
estimate of number of spiders that are consumed in this way in the 
UK each year?

- $600000$
- $7000000$
- $80000000$
- $900000000$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of spiders consumed per year} &= \dfrac {60000000 \times 8} {70} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {480000000} {70} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {48000000 \cancel 0} {7 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {48000000} {7} \\\\
                                           &= 6857142 \text{ remainder } 6 \\\\
                                           &\approx 7000000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$7000000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of spiders consumed per year} &= \dfrac {60000000 \times 8} {70} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {480000000} {70} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {48000000 \cancel 0} {7 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {48000000} {7} \\\\
                                           &= 6857142 \text{ remainder } 6 \\\\
                                           &\approx 7000000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7000000$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

I choose three numbers from this number
square, including one number from each row
and one number from each column.

| $1$ | $2$ | $3$ |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| $4$ | $5$ | $6$ |
| $7$ | $8$ | $9$ |

I then multiply the three numbers together.

What is the largest possible product?

- $96$
- $105$
- $162$
- $504$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

|    Possible triplets    | Product |
|:-----------------------:|:-------:|
|  $1 \times 5 \times 9$  |   $45$  |
|  $1 \times 8 \times 6$  |   $48$  |
|  $4 \times 2 \times 9$  |   $72$  |
|  $4 \times 8 \times 3$  |   $96$  |
|  $7 \times 5 \times 3$  |  $105$  |
|  $7 \times 2 \times 6$  |   $84$  |

Largest possible product $= 105$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$105$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>|    Possible triplets    | Product |
|:-----------------------:|:-------:|
|  $1 \times 5 \times 9$  |   $45$  |
|  $1 \times 8 \times 6$  |   $48$  |
|  $4 \times 2 \times 9$  |   $72$  |
|  $4 \times 8 \times 3$  |   $96$  |
|  $7 \times 5 \times 3$  |  $105$  |
|  $7 \times 2 \times 6$  |   $84$  |
<emptyline>
Largest possible product $= 105$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$105$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

In the British Army there are $4500$ men who are taller than $180 \ \text{cms}$. 
Of these $750$ are taller than $190 \ \text{cms}$.

How many men are there who are between $180$ and $190 \ \text{cms}$ tall? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of men between 180 and 190}         &= \text{Men taller than 180} - \text{Men taller than 190} \\\\
                                                 &= 4500 - 750 \\\\
                                                 &= 3750
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3750$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of men between 180 and 190}         &= \text{Men taller than 180} - \text{Men taller than 190} \\\\
                                                 &= 4500 - 750 \\\\
                                                 &= 3750
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3750$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>3F6998ED-9AC1-46D3-A19F-E85725A6237B</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>forest-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-3:1:7</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Jordan eats $\dfrac{1}{4}$ of a pizza and Anna eats $\dfrac{1}{3}$ of what is left.

Rohit, Guhesh and Matilda share equally the remainder.

What fraction of the pizza did Matilda eat? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Anna ate}    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {of} \ \left(1 - \dfrac {1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {1 \times 4} {1 \times 4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {4} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {4 - 1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {3 \times 4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3} \times 4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
\text {Pizza left}  &= 1 - \dfrac {1} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 4} {1 \times 4} - \dfrac {1} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4} {4} -  \dfrac {1} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 - 1 - 1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Matilda gets} &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {6}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {6}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Anna ate}    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {of} \ \left(1 - \dfrac {1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {1 \times 4} {1 \times 4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {4} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \left( \dfrac {4 - 1} {4} \right) \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {3 \times 4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3} \times 4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
\text {Pizza left}  &= 1 - \dfrac {1} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 4} {1 \times 4} - \dfrac {1} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4} {4} -  \dfrac {1} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 - 1 - 1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Matilda gets} &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {6}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {6}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>32AD190F-BC19-4308-B41D-C508037F0B80</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>forest-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-3:1:12</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Grace plants a row of daffodils.

There is a gap of $1.1 \ \text {metres}$ within each daffodil, and the row begins and ends with a daffodil.

She has $8$ daffodils.

How long is the row of daffodils when she has finished? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

<div class='question question'>

Jerry the bearded dragon eats $12$ locusts for breakfast at the start of every day.

His owner buys $100$ locusts one afternoon for Jerry to eat.

How many locusts does Jerry get on the day these locusts run out? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}       &= \dfrac {100} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 25} {4 \times 3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel 4 \times 3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25} {3} \\\\
                            &= 8 \text{ remainder } 1 \\\\
                            &\approx 8
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of locusts on 8th day}                     &= 100 - 8 \times 12 \\\\
                                                        &= 100 - 96 \\\\
                                                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}       &= \dfrac {100} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 25} {4 \times 3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel 4 \times 3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {25} {3} \\\\
                            &= 8 \text{ remainder } 1 \\\\
                            &\approx 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of locusts on 8th day}                     &= 100 - 8 \times 12 \\\\
                                                        &= 100 - 96 \\\\
                                                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A number is a multiple of $11$ if $11$ goes into it. $33$ and $121$ are both multiples of $11$.

Ajay says that a number is a multiple of $11$ if the difference of alternate sums of digits in the number is a multiple of $11$.

So, $27390628$ is a multiple of $11$ because

$2 + 3 + 0 + 2 = 8$

$7 + 9 + 6 + 8 = 30$

and $30 - 8 = 22$ which is a multiple of $11$. 

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Is $978261$ a multiple of $11?$ Show your working clearly.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of digits at odd position}      &= 9 + 8 + 6 \\\\
                                          &= 23 \\\\
\text{Sum of digits at even position}     &= 7 + 2 + 1 \\\\
                                          &= 10 \\\\
\text{Difference}                         &= 23 - 10 \\\\
                                          &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

$13$ is not a multiple of $11$.

Therefore $978261$ is also not a multiple of $11$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

No

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of digits at odd position}      &= 9 + 8 + 6 \\\\
                                          &= 23 \\\\
\text{Sum of digits at even position}     &= 7 + 2 + 1 \\\\
                                          &= 10 \\\\
\text{Difference}                         &= 23 - 10 \\\\
                                          &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$13$ is not a multiple of $11$.
<emptyline>
Therefore $978261$ is also not a multiple of $11$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find one of the ways to fill in the gaps so that this number is a multiple of $11$.

$\Large{7 \ \text{\textunderscore} \ 5  \ 6 \ \text{\textunderscore} \ 0}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Number after filling the gaps $= 725670$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of digits at odd position}      &= 7 + 5 + 7 \\\\
                                          &= 19 \\\\
\text{Sum of digits at even position}     &= 2 + 6 + 0 \\\\
                                          &= 8 \\\\
\text{Difference}                         &= 19 - 8 \\\\
                                          &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

$11$ is a multiple of $11$

Therefore $725670$ is also a multiple of $11$.

</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>FB6C0594-DADA-4D15-ACD2-FD4DCB46FFA0</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>forest-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:6</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Krishn has $1 \ \text{hour}$ to clean the house before his parents get back from their holidays.

He has to hoover $3$ rooms, clean out his pet hamster's cage and wipe off the drawing of an elephant he accidently did on the bathroom wall.

Each room takes $12  \ \text{minutes}$ to hoover, it takes $18  \ \text{minutes}$ to clean out his hamster's cage and $21  \ \text{minutes}$ to rub out the elephant, which he does last.

Wht fraction of the elephant is left on the wall when Krishn's parents arrive home?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time left to wipe off the elephant}       &= 60 - 12 \times 3 - 18 \\\\
                                                &= 60 - 36 - 18 \\\\
                                                &= 60 - 54 \\\\
                                                &= 6 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of elephant wiped off}           &= \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
\text{Fraction of elephant left}                &= 1 - \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1 \times 21} {1 \times 21} - \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {21} {21} - \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {21 - 6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {15} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {7 \times 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 3} {7 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5} {7}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {5} {7}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time left to wipe off the elephant}       &= 60 - 12 \times 3 - 18 \\\\
                                                &= 60 - 36 - 18 \\\\
                                                &= 60 - 54 \\\\
                                                &= 6 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of elephant wiped off}           &= \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
\text{Fraction of elephant left}                &= 1 - \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1 \times 21} {1 \times 21} - \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {21} {21} - \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {21 - 6} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {15} {21} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {7 \times 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 3} {7 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5} {7}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {5} {7}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

One quire of paper is equal to $24$ sheets.
One ream is equal to $20$ quires.
How many sheets are there in $2$ ream?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of sheets in two ream}    &= 2 \times 20 \times 24 \\\\
                                        &= 40 \times 24 \\\\
                                        &= 960
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$960$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of sheets in two ream}    &= 2 \times 20 \times 24 \\\\
                                        &= 40 \times 24 \\\\
                                        &= 960
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>E831F3CD-F836-4E5D-BFC4-F0866B4F7900</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>forest-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:27</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Milly has some marbles at the start of the week.

One day at school she does well in her marble games with her friends and increase her total by a tenth.

The next day is not so good and she loses a tenth that time.

Has she ended up with more, less or the same as she had at the start of the week? Explain your answer carefully.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of marbles Milly had at the start of the week $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total marbles at the end of first day}     &= a + 10 \% \ \text{of} \ a \\\\
                                                 &= a + \dfrac {10} {100} \times a \\\\
                                                 &= a + \dfrac {10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {a \times 100} {1 \times 100} + \dfrac {10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {100a} {100} + \dfrac {10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {100a + 10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {110a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11 \cancel 0a} {10 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} \\\\
\text{Total marbles at the end of second day}    &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - 10 \% \ \text{of} \ \dfrac {11a} {10} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {10} {100} \times \dfrac {11a} {10} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {10 \times 11a} {100 \times 10} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {\cancel {10} \times 11a} {100 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a \times 10} {10 \times 10} - \dfrac {11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {110a} {100} - \dfrac {11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {110a - 11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {99a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= 0.99a
\end{aligned}
$

She ended up with less marbles.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Less

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of marbles Milly had at the start of the week $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total marbles at the end of first day}     &= a + 10 \% \ \text{of} \ a \\\\
                                                 &= a + \dfrac {10} {100} \times a \\\\
                                                 &= a + \dfrac {10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {a \times 100} {1 \times 100} + \dfrac {10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {100a} {100} + \dfrac {10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {100a + 10a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {110a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11 \cancel 0a} {10 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} \\\\
\text{Total marbles at the end of second day}    &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - 10 \% \ \text{of} \ \dfrac {11a} {10} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {10} {100} \times \dfrac {11a} {10} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {10 \times 11a} {100 \times 10} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {\cancel {10} \times 11a} {100 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a} {10} - \dfrac {11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {11a \times 10} {10 \times 10} - \dfrac {11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {110a} {100} - \dfrac {11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {110a - 11a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {99a} {100} \\\\
                                                 &= 0.99a
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
She ended up with less marbles.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Less
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Cheese costs $\pounds 5.40 \ \text{per kg}$. Jessica buys $0.6\ \text{kg}$ of cheese. How much does Jessica pay? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount Jessica paid}      &= 5.40 \times 0.6 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {54} {10} \times \dfrac {6} {10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {54 \times 6} {10 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {324} {100} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 3.24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 3.24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                &= \dfrac {54 \times 6} {10 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {324} {100} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 3.24
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

There are $31 \ \text{days}$ in July. Each day throughout July I picked $300\ \text{g}$ of raspberries from my garden.

What was the total weight of the raspberries I picked that month? Give your answer in $\text{kilograms}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of the raspberries}          &= 31 \times \dfrac {300} {1000} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {31 \times 300} {1000} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {9300} {1000} \\\\
                                                &= 9.3 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9.3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of the raspberries}          &= 31 \times \dfrac {300} {1000} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {31 \times 300} {1000} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {9300} {1000} \\\\
                                                &= 9.3 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9.3 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>31FC660E-D13F-432D-A6ED-CC48AC82481E</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-2:1:20</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Natalie, Sara and Antonia catch $3$ different buses to go to the same destination.

Antonia catches the $19{:}15$ bus.

Natalie's bus journey takes three times as long as Sara's. 
Natalie's bus leaves $20 \ \text{minutes}$ before Antonia's bus and arrives at $20{:}10$. 
Sara catches the $19{:}25$ bus. 

What time does Sara arrive at the destination? 

![james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-20-00.png](/assets/james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-20-00.png "james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-20-00.png") 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time when Natalia catch the bus}           &= \text{Time when Antonia catch the bus} - 20 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                                 &= 19{:}15 - 20 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                                 &= 18{:}55 \\\\
\text{Duration of Natalia journey}               &= 20{:}10 - 18{:}55 \\\\
                                                 &= 75 \ \text{min} \\\\
\text{Duration of Sara journey}                  &= \dfrac {\text{Duration of Natalia journey}} {3} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {75} {3} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {3 \times 25} {3} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 25} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                                 &= 25 \ \text{min} \\\\
\text{Time when Sara arrive at destination}      &= 19{:}25 + 25 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                                 &= 19{:}50
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19{:}50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time when Natalia catch the bus}           &= \text{Time when Antonia catch the bus} - 20 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                                 &= 19{:}15 - 20 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                                 &= 18{:}55 \\\\
\text{Duration of Natalia journey}               &= 20{:}10 - 18{:}55 \\\\
                                                 &= 75 \ \text{min} \\\\
\text{Duration of Sara journey}                  &= \dfrac {\text{Duration of Natalia journey}} {3} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {75} {3} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {3 \times 25} {3} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 25} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                                 &= 25 \ \text{min} \\\\
\text{Time when Sara arrive at destination}      &= 19{:}25 + 25 \ \text{min} \\\\
                                                 &= 19{:}50
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19{:}50$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:13</p>

<div class='question question'>

Sara starts her maths homework at $4.30 \ \text{pm}$ and she finishes it at $6.10 \ \text{pm}$. 
During that time she watches 'Neighbours' for $25 \ \text{minutes}$ and 
she speaks to her friend Jean on the telephone for $20 \ \text{minutes}$. 
How long is Sara actually working on her maths?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total time}                             &= 6:10 \ \text{pm} - 4:30 \ \text{pm} \\\\
                                              &= 100 \ \text{min} \\\\
\text{Time spent on homework}                 &= 100 - 25 - 20 \\\\
                                              &= 100 - 45 \\\\
                                              &= 55 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$55 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total time}                             &= 6:10 \ \text{pm} - 4:30 \ \text{pm} \\\\
                                              &= 100 \ \text{min} \\\\
\text{Time spent on homework}                 &= 100 - 25 - 20 \\\\
                                              &= 100 - 45 \\\\
                                              &= 55 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$55 \ \text{min}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The total of the opposite faces on a dice is always $7$.
Jack places  three dice on a table as shown in the diagram. 
What is the total score of the faces that Jack cannot see?

![james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-19-00.png](/assets/james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-19-00.png "james-allens-girls-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-19-00.png")  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total score of the faces that Jack cannot see}     &= 3 \times ((7 -6) + (7 - 3) + (7 - 2)) + 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                                         &= 3 \times (1 + 4 + 5) + 4 \\\\
                                                         &= 3 \times 10 + 4 \\\\
                                                         &= 30 + 4 \\\\
                                                         &= 34
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$34$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total score of the faces that Jack cannot see}     &= 3 \times ((7 -6) + (7 - 3) + (7 - 2)) + 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                                         &= 3 \times (1 + 4 + 5) + 4 \\\\
                                                         &= 3 \times 10 + 4 \\\\
                                                         &= 30 + 4 \\\\
                                                         &= 34
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$34$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A length of rope is $5 \ \text{m}$ long. It is cut into four unequal lengths. Three of the pieces are $147 \ \text{cm}$, $132.5 \ \text{cm}$ and $67 \ \text{cm}$. How long is the fourth piece? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Length of fourth piece} &= 5 \ \text {m} - (147 \ \text {cm} + 132.5 \ \text {cm} + 67 \ \text {cm}) \\\\
                               &= 5 \ \text {m} - 346.5 \ \text {cm} \\\\
                               &= 5 \ \text {m} - \dfrac {346.5} {100} \ \text {m} \\\\
                               &= 5 - 3.465 \\\\
                               &= 1.535 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.535 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Length of fourth piece} &= 5 \ \text {m} - (147 \ \text {cm} + 132.5 \ \text {cm} + 67 \ \text {cm}) \\\\
                               &= 5 \ \text {m} - 346.5 \ \text {cm} \\\\
                               &= 5 \ \text {m} - \dfrac {346.5} {100} \ \text {m} \\\\
                               &= 5 - 3.465 \\\\
                               &= 1.535 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.535 \ \text {m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Tom is $142 \ \text{cm}$ tall and Harry is $168 \ \text{cm}$ tall. 
James is half way between Tom's and Harry's height. 
Work out James' height. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {James' height} &= \dfrac {\text {Tom's height} + \text {Harry's height}} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {142 + 168} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {310} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {155 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {155 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                      &= 155 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$155 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {James' height} &= \dfrac {\text {Tom's height} + \text {Harry's height}} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {142 + 168} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {310} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {155 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {155 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                      &= 155 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$155 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$3$ masses are measured to be $720 \ \text{g}$, $3.46 \ \text{kg}$, and $2 \ \text{kg } 53 \ \text{g}$. 
What is their total mass, give your answer in grams. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total mass} &= 720 \ \text{g} + 3.46 \ \text{kg} + 2 \ \text{kg} \ 53 \ \text{g} \\\\
                   &= 720 \ \text{g} + 3.46 \times 1000 \ \text{g} + 2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} + 53 \ \text{g} \\\\
                   &= 720 + 3.46 \times 1000 + 2 \times 1000 + 53 \\\\
                   &= 720 + 3460 + 2000 + 53 \\\\
                   &= 6233 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6233 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total mass} &= 720 \ \text{g} + 3.46 \ \text{kg} + 2 \ \text{kg} \ 53 \ \text{g} \\\\
                   &= 720 \ \text{g} + 3.46 \times 1000 \ \text{g} + 2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} + 53 \ \text{g} \\\\
                   &= 720 + 3.46 \times 1000 + 2 \times 1000 + 53 \\\\
                   &= 720 + 3460 + 2000 + 53 \\\\
                   &= 6233 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6233 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

![kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-00.png](/assets/kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-00.png "kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-00.png")     ![kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-01.png](/assets/kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-01.png "kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-01.png")     ![kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-02.png](/assets/kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-02.png "kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-02.png")

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Nine people sit at one table. They share the chocolate equally.

![kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-1-00.png "kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-1-00.png")

What fraction of a bar do they each have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Fraction per person}  &= \dfrac {\text {Total bars}} {\text {Total people}} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {3 \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Fraction per person}  &= \dfrac {\text {Total bars}} {\text {Total people}} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {3 \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

![kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-2-00.png "kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$

$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                   &= \dfrac {3} {4}

$

</div>

<div class='answers'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In another room there are two tables.

There are four bars of chocolate on one table. There are five on the other.

![kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-3-00.png](/assets/kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-3-00.png "kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-3-00.png") ![kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-3-01.png](/assets/kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-3-01.png "kent-college--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-18-subquestion-3-01.png")

Twelve people sit at the table with four bars. Some other people sit at the table with five bars.

At each table they share the chocolate equally. Everyone in the room has the same fraction of a bar.

How many people sit at the table with five bars?

Show your working.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of people sitting at the table with five bars $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {4} {12}                                                         &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
4a                                                                      &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {4} \\\\
a                                                                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                                                                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                                                                       &= 5 \times 3 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 15
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of people sitting at the table with five bars $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {4} {12}                                                         &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
4a                                                                      &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {4} \\\\
a                                                                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                                                                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                                                                       &= 5 \times 3 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 15
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>kent-college-canterbury--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1:1:13</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

A number line starts at $-9$ and finishes at $11$.

What number is $\dfrac{2}{5}$ of the way along the number line?

![kent-college-canterbury--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-13-00.png](/assets/kent-college-canterbury--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-13-00.png "kent-college-canterbury--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-13-00.png")

Show your working. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Effective length of the number line} &= 11 - (-9) \\\\
                                           &= 11 + 9 \\\\
                                           &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Position of required number from start}    &= \dfrac {2} {5} \times 20 \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 20} {5} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 5 \times 4} {5} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {5} \times 4} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 \times 4 \\\\
                                                 &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number}        &= - 9 + 8 \\\\
                     &= - 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$- 1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Effective length of the number line} &= 11 - (-9) \\\\
                                           &= 11 + 9 \\\\
                                           &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Position of required number from start}    &= \dfrac {2} {5} \times 20 \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 20} {5} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 5 \times 4} {5} \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {5} \times 4} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 \times 4 \\\\
                                                 &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number}        &= - 9 + 8 \\\\
                     &= - 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$- 1$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Bill has a bag containing $60$ sweets. He eats a half of the sweets himself and then gives away a third of the sweets that remain. How many sweets does Bill have left?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Sweets left}  &= \text {Total sweets} - (\text {Sweets eaten} + \text {Sweets given away}) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {of} \ 60 + \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {of} \ \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {of} \ 60 \bigg) \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 +\dfrac {1} {3} \times \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \bigg) \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {60} {2} + \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {60} {2} \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} + \dfrac {10 \times 3 \times 2} {3 \times 2} \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} + \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - (30 + 10) \\\\
                     &= 60 - 40 \\\\
                     &= 20
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Sweets left}  &= \text {Total sweets} - (\text {Sweets eaten} + \text {Sweets given away}) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {of} \ 60 + \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {of} \ \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {of} \ 60 \bigg) \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 +\dfrac {1} {3} \times \bigg(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \bigg) \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {60} {2} + \dfrac {1} {3} \times \dfrac {60} {2} \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} + \dfrac {10 \times 3 \times 2} {3 \times 2} \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - \bigg(\dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} + \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \bigg) \\\\
                     &= 60 - (30 + 10) \\\\
                     &= 60 - 40 \\\\
                     &= 20
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The total running time for a film is $100 \ \text{minutes}$.

$3\%$ of the time is taken up with the opening title sequence and final credits.

A total of $25 \ \text{minutes}$ of the film is shot outdoors.

One-fifth of the film is shot inside a hospital.

$0.17$ of the film is shot in a greenhouse.

All the remainder of the film is shot inside The Manor House.


How much of the film is shot inside The Manor House?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOANSWER
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Answer as

A fraction:

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Duration of film shot inside The Manor House} &= (100 - 3 \% \ \text{of} \ 100 - 25 - \dfrac {1} {5} \ \text{of} \ 100 - 0.17 \ \text{of} \ 100) \\\\
                                                    &= 100 - \dfrac {3} {100} \times 100 - 25 - \dfrac {1} {5} \times 100 - \dfrac {17} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                                    &= 100 - 25 - \dfrac {3 \times 100} {100} - \dfrac {100} {5} - \dfrac {17 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                                    &= 75 - \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} - \dfrac {5 \times 20} {5} - \dfrac {17 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                                    &= 75 - 3 - 17 - \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 20} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                                    &= 75 - 20 - 20 \\\\
                                                    &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of film shot inside The Manor House} &= \dfrac {35} {100} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {7 \times 5} {20 \times 5} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel 5} {20 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {7} {20}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {7} {20}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                                    &= 100 - \dfrac {3} {100} \times 100 - 25 - \dfrac {1} {5} \times 100 - \dfrac {17} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                                    &= 100 - 25 - \dfrac {3 \times 100} {100} - \dfrac {100} {5} - \dfrac {17 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                                    &= 75 - \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} - \dfrac {5 \times 20} {5} - \dfrac {17 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                                    &= 75 - 3 - 17 - \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 20} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                                    &= 75 - 20 - 20 \\\\
                                                    &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of film shot inside The Manor House} &= \dfrac {35} {100} \\\\

$
</code></pre>

- $152$
- $168$
- $197$
- $199$
- $201$

</div>

Prime numbers between $30$ and $50$ $= 31, 37, 41, 43, 47$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of numbers}               &= 31 + 37 + 41 + 43 + 47 \\\\
                                    &= 68 + 41 + 90 \\\\
                                    &= 109 + 90 \\\\
                                    &= 199
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

$199$

<div class='question question'>

Two positive whole numbers add together to make $23$. What is the smallest possible answer when the two numbers are multiplied together? 

- $132$ 
- $1$ 
- $42$ 
- $22$ 
- $23$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest possible answer} &= 22 \times 1 \\\\
                                &= 22
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$22$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest possible answer} &= 22 \times 1 \\\\
                                &= 22
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$22$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What result does Tess get when she starts with the number $732?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 7 ^ 2 + 3 ^ 2 + 2 ^ 2 \\\\
&= 49 + 9 + 4 \\\\
&= 62
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$62$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 7 ^ 2 + 3 ^ 2 + 2 ^ 2 \\\\
&= 49 + 9 + 4 \\\\
&= 62
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$62$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

&= 25

$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Explain why it's not possible to find a two-digit number which gives Tess a result of $14$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

For result $= 14$ we need atleast $3$ digits

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1 ^ 2 + 2 ^ 2 + 3 ^ 2 \\\\
&= 1 + 4 + 9 \\\\
&= 14
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore it is not possible to find a two digit number which will give result $= 14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

%empty%

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1 ^ 2 + 2 ^ 2 + 3 ^ 2 \\\\
&= 1 + 4 + 9 \\\\
&= 14
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore it is not possible to find a two digit number which will give result $= 14$
</code></pre>

<div class='papername'>

tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A box of $92$ Smarties contains only reef, green and purple Smarties. If there are $37$ red Smarties and $29$ green Smarties how many purple Smarties does it contain?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Purple Smarties}  &= \text {Total Smarties} - (\text{Red Smarties} + \text{Green Smarties}) \\\\
                         &= 92 - (37 + 29) \\\\
                         &= 92 - 66 \\\\
                         &= 26
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$26$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Purple Smarties}  &= \text {Total Smarties} - (\text{Red Smarties} + \text{Green Smarties}) \\\\
                         &= 92 - (37 + 29) \\\\
                         &= 92 - 66 \\\\
                         &= 26
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$26$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Jenny is given $3$ large jigsaw puzzles for Christmas which get muddled together. The first contains $496$ pieces, the second $812$ pieces and the third $984$ pieces.

By rounding each of these numbers to the nearest $100$, write down a simple sum you can do to estimate the total number of jigsaw pieces there are all together.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Approximate number of jigsaw pieces}      &= 500 + 800 + 1000 \\\\
                                                &= 2300
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2300$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Approximate number of jigsaw pieces}      &= 500 + 800 + 1000 \\\\
                                                &= 2300
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2300$
</code></pre>

A length of ribbon is $240\ \text{cm}$ long. It is cut into $6$ pieces of equal length. What is the length of each piece?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Length of each piece}     &= \dfrac {240} {6} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {6 \times 40} {6} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 40} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                &= 40 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

                                &= \dfrac {6 \times 40} {6} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 40} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                &= 40 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much taller is Meera than Ellie? Give your answer in centimetres.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Meera is taller than Ellie by} &= 1.62 \ \text{m} - 1.39 \ \text{m} \\\\
                                     &= 1.62 \times 100 \ \text{cm} - 1.39 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                                     &= 162 - 139 \\\\
                                     &= 23 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$23 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$

                                     &= 1.62 \times 100 \ \text{cm} - 1.39 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                                     &= 162 - 139 \\\\
                                     &= 23 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Meera is now exactly $3$ times as tall as she was when she was born. What was her height in centimetres when she was born?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Meera's height when she was born}   &= \dfrac {1.62} {3} \ \text{m} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {1.62 \times 100} {3} \ \text{cm} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {162} {3} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {54 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {54 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                                          &= 54 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$54 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                          &= \dfrac {1.62 \times 100} {3} \ \text{cm} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {162} {3} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {54 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {54 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                                          &= 54 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much does it cost for $7$ pencils bought individually?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost for 7 pencils} &= 7 \times 15 \\\\
                          &= 105 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$105 \ \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost for 7 pencils} &= 7 \times 15 \\\\
                          &= 105 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
