</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If I buy $4$ boxes of $24$ pencils, how much change should I receive if I pay with a $\pounds20$ note?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Change received} &= 20 - 4 \times 3.12 \\\\
                       &= 20 - 4 \times \dfrac {312} {100} \\\\
                       &= 20 - \dfrac {4 \times 312} {100} \\\\
                       &= 20 - \dfrac {1248} {100} \\\\
                       &= 20 - 12.48 \\\\
                       &= \pounds 7.52
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 7.52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Change received} &= 20 - 4 \times 3.12 \\\\
                       &= 20 - 4 \times \dfrac {312} {100} \\\\
                       &= 20 - \dfrac {4 \times 312} {100} \\\\
                       &= 20 - \dfrac {1248} {100} \\\\
                       &= 20 - 12.48 \\\\
                       &= \pounds 7.52
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 7.52$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much is saved by buying a box of $24$ rather than buying $24$ pencils individually?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money saved} &= 24 \times 15 \ \text{p} - \pounds 3.12 \\\\
                   &= 360 \ \text{p} - 3.12 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
                   &= 360 - 3.12 \times 100 \\\\
                   &= 360 - 312 \\\\
                   &= 48 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \ \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money saved} &= 24 \times 15 \ \text{p} - \pounds 3.12 \\\\
                   &= 360 \ \text{p} - 3.12 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
                   &= 360 - 3.12 \times 100 \\\\
                   &= 360 - 312 \\\\
                   &= 48 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48 \ \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A school requires enough pencils so that each of its $340$ pupils can have a pencil each. How many boxes of $24$ pencils should the school buy, and how many spare pencils will this leave?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boxes required}         &= \dfrac {340} {24} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {85 \times 4} {6 \times 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {85 \times \cancel 4} {6 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {85} {6} \\\\
                                        &= 14 \text{ remainder } 1 \\\\
                                        &\approx 15 \\\\
\text{Number of spare pencils}          &= 15 \times 24 - 340\\\\
                                        &= 360 - 340 \\\\
                                        &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Number of boxes $= 15$

Number of spare pencils $= 20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boxes required}         &= \dfrac {340} {24} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {85 \times 4} {6 \times 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {85 \times \cancel 4} {6 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {85} {6} \\\\
                                        &= 14 \text{ remainder } 1 \\\\
                                        &\approx 15 \\\\
\text{Number of spare pencils}          &= 15 \times 24 - 340\\\\
                                        &= 360 - 340 \\\\
                                        &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Number of boxes $= 15$

Number of spare pencils $= 20$
</code></pre>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3:1:10</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

tsimpleword
</li>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

A ball of string is $2\ \text{metres}$ long. Pieces of length $30\ \text{cm}$ are cut from the ball. How many whole pieces may be cut and what length is left over?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pieces} &= \dfrac {\text{Length of string}} {\text{Length of a piece}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \ \text{m}} {30  \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} {30 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 10 \cancel {0}} {3 \cancel {0}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {3} \\\\
                        &= 6 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
                        &\approx 6 \\\\
\text{Remaining length}     &= 2 \ \text{m} - 6 \times 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                            &= 2 \times 100 \ \text{cm} - 6 \times 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                            &= 200 - 180 \\\\
                            &= 20 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6, 20 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pieces} &= \dfrac {\text{Length of string}} {\text{Length of a piece}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \ \text{m}} {30  \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} {30 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 10 \cancel {0}} {3 \cancel {0}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20} {3} \\\\
                        &= 6 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
                        &\approx 6 \\\\
\text{Remaining length}     &= 2 \ \text{m} - 6 \times 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                            &= 2 \times 100 \ \text{cm} - 6 \times 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                            &= 200 - 180 \\\\
                            &= 20 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6, 20 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A glass holds $225\ \text{ml}$.

![leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-6-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-6-subquestion-1-00.png "leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-6-subquestion-1-00.png")

An adult needs about $1.8\ \text{litres}$ of water each day to stay healthy.

How many glasses is that? 

Show your working.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount of water needed per day}     &= 1.8 \ \text{litres} \\\\
                                          &= 1.8 \times 1000 \ \text{ml} \\\\
                                          &= 1.8 \times 1000 \\\\
                                          &= 1800 \ \text{ml} \\\\
\text{Number of glasses of water}         &= \dfrac {1800} {225} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {8 \times 225} {225} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel {225}} {\cancel {225}} \\\\
                                          &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount of water needed per day}     &= 1.8 \ \text{litres} \\\\
                                          &= 1.8 \times 1000 \ \text{ml} \\\\
                                          &= 1.8 \times 1000 \\\\
                                          &= 1800 \ \text{ml} \\\\
\text{Number of glasses of water}         &= \dfrac {1800} {225} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {8 \times 225} {225} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel {225}} {\cancel {225}} \\\\
                                          &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

An adult weighs $80\ \text{kg}$.

$60\%$ of his total mass is water.

What is the mass of this water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of water}        &= 60 \% \ \text{of} \ 80 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {60} {100} \times 80 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {60 \times 80} {100} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {6 \cancel {0} \times 8 \cancel {0}} {1 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} \\\\
                            &= 6 \times 8 \\\\
                            &= 48 \ \text {kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \ \text {kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of water}        &= 60 \% \ \text{of} \ 80 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {60} {100} \times 80 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {60 \times 80} {100} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {6 \cancel {0} \times 8 \cancel {0}} {1 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} \\\\
                            &= 6 \times 8 \\\\
                            &= 48 \ \text {kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48 \ \text {kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
